### System Node Endpoint: https://learner.saveetha.in
### Analysis of the Student Portal Section

#### 1. Action or Transactional Feature it Controls
The section in question appears to be a part of a student portal, likely for managing and viewing course schedules, attendance records, and other related activities. The specific page shown is likely designed to display the student's schedule for a particular month, in this case, August 2026. The features it controls include:

- **Viewing Course Schedules**: The page lists courses the student is enrolled in, along with their respective slots, lesson times, and venues.
- **Viewing Attendance Records**: There are buttons labeled "VIEW ATTENDANCE" next to each course, which likely allow the student to view their attendance records for each course.
- **Circulars and Workflow Requests**: The page also shows a count of circulars (likely announcements or important notices) and workflow requests, which the student can access for further information or actions.

#### 2. Variables, Parameters, Text Grids, or Functions it Exposes
The page exposes several key variables and parameters that are relevant to the student's schedule and attendance:

- **Course Details**: Each course has specific details such as the course code (e.g., 19AI305), course name (e.g., "Advanced C Programming"), slot number (e.g., 260D1171), lesson time (e.g., 08:00 AM - 09:59 AM), and venue (e.g., 2872).
- **Attendance Records**: The "VIEW ATTENDANCE" button likely leads to a page where the student can view their attendance records for each course.
- **Circulars and Workflow Requests**: The number of circulars and workflow requests is displayed, indicating the student can access these announcements or requests.

#### 3. How this Page Passes Information to the Wider Web System Structure
The page appears to be part of a larger web system that manages student schedules and activities. Here’s how it likely interacts with the wider system:

- **User Authentication**: The student is logged in with their ID number (25013957) and name (MUHAMMAD ASJAD E). This information is likely stored in a database and used to authenticate the user.
- **Course Scheduling**: The course details are likely stored in a database and retrieved based on the student's enrollment status. The page dynamically populates this information based on the student's enrollment.
- **Attendance Records**: Attendance records are likely stored in a separate database and accessed by the student through the "VIEW ATTENDANCE" button. This button likely triggers a query to the database to retrieve the attendance records.
- **Circulars and Workflow Requests**: Circulars and workflow requests are likely stored in a separate database and accessed by the student through the "VIEW ATTENDANCE" button. This button likely triggers a query to the database to retrieve the circulars and workflow requests.

### Summary
The page in question is a part of a student portal that manages and displays the student's schedule, attendance records, and other related activities. It exposes variables such as course details, attendance records, and circulars/workflow requests. The page interacts with the wider web system by retrieving and displaying this information based on the student's enrollment status and access rights.

- **Backend Processing**: The backend processes the form submission and updates the user's notification preferences in the database. This ensures that the user's preferences are stored and can be accessed in the future.
- **Webhook Integration**: The backend might also integrate with webhooks to notify other systems or services about the user's notification preferences. For example, if the user's preferences change, the backend could send a notification to a third-party system or service.

### System Node Endpoint: https://learner.saveetha.in/people/active-sessions/
### Analysis of the Student Portal's Active Sessions Page

#### 1. What Action or Transactional Feature it Controls:
The "Active Sessions" page is designed to manage and monitor the sessions of users who are currently logged into the student portal. It allows administrators to review and control sessions, including ending sessions, invalidating sessions, and managing sessions for specific users. This feature is crucial for maintaining security and ensuring that only authorized users have access to the system.

#### 2. The Variables, Parameters, Text Grids, or Functions it Exposes:
- **Session ID**: Each session is assigned a unique ID, which is used to identify and manage the session.
- **Browser/Device**: Information about the browser and device used to access the session.
- **Session Started**: The date and time when the session was initiated.
- **Last Accessed**: The date and time when the session was last accessed.
- **IP Address**: The IP address of the device from which the session was accessed.
- **Action**: Options to either "Use Logout to end this session" or "Invalidate" the session.
- **User ID**: The user ID associated with the session, which is used to identify the user.
- **User Name**: The name of the user associated with the session.
- **User Email**: The email address of the user associated with the session.
- **User Role**: The role of the user (e.g., student, administrator).
- **Session Status**: Indicates whether the session is active or inactive.

#### 3. How This Page Passes Information to the Wider Web System Structure:
- **Session Management**: The page uses session IDs to manage and track sessions. When a user logs in, a session ID is generated and stored in the session cookie. This session ID is used throughout the session to identify the user.
- **User Authentication**: The page checks the session ID against the session cookie to ensure that the user is authenticated and authorized to access the session.
- **User Data**: The page retrieves user data from the database using the session ID. This data includes user information such as name, email, and role.
- **Session Control**: The page allows administrators to control sessions by using the "Use Logout to end this session" and "Invalidate" options. These actions are typically handled by server-side scripts that update the session status in the database.
- **IP Address Tracking**: The page logs the IP address of the device accessing the session, which can be used for security and troubleshooting purposes.
- **User Tracking**: The page tracks user activity by recording the date and time of the last access, which can be used for reporting and analytics.

### Summary
The "Active Sessions" page is a critical component of the student portal that manages and controls user sessions. It exposes various variables and parameters related to user sessions, including session IDs, browser/device information, and user data. The page interacts with the wider web system structure by managing session status, tracking user activity, and allowing administrators to control sessions. This functionality is essential for maintaining security, managing user access, and providing analytics.

### System Node Endpoint: https://learner.saveetha.in/academics/circulars/
### Analysis of the Student Portal Section

#### 1. Action or Transactional Feature it Controls
The section in question is the "Circulars" page, which is designed to manage and track various circular communications, task progress, mentor verification, and manager approvals. This page allows users to view, filter, and manage different circulars based on specific criteria such as title, role, and date range.

#### 2. Variables, Parameters, Text Grids, or Functions it Exposes
- **Title**: A text input field where users can search for a partial title of the circular.
- **Role**: A dropdown menu that allows users to filter the circulars based on their role (e.g., Participant, Mentor, Manager).
- **From Date**: A date input field that users can specify the start date of the circular.
- **To Date**: A date input field that users can specify the end date of the circular.
- **Info**: A checkbox that, when checked, displays additional information about the circular.
- **Notification**: A checkbox that, when checked, sends a notification about the circular.
- **Apply**: A button that applies the selected filters and displays the filtered circulars.

#### 3. How this Page Passes Information to the Wider Web System Structure
The "Circulars" page interacts with the wider web system structure through the following mechanisms:

- **Form Submission**: When the user clicks the "Apply" button, the form data is submitted to the server. This data includes the title, role, from date, to date, and checkboxes.
- **Server-side Processing**: The server processes the submitted data and retrieves the corresponding circulars from its database based on the specified filters.
- **Data Display**: The server sends the filtered circulars back to the client-side, which then displays them in the UI.
- **User Interaction**: Users can interact with the UI to filter and view the circulars, and the server updates the database accordingly.

### Detailed Breakdown

#### Form Submission
When the user clicks the "Apply" button, the form data is submitted to the server. The form data includes:
- **Title**: Partial title of the circular.
- **Role**: Role of the user (e.g., Participant, Mentor, Manager).
- **From Date**: Start date of the circular.
- **To Date**: End date of the circular.
- **Info**: Whether to display additional information about the circular.
- **Notification**: Whether to send a notification about the circular.

#### Server-side Processing
The server processes the submitted data and retrieves the corresponding circulars from its database based on the specified filters. For example, if the user filters by title, the server would search the database for circulars that match the partial title entered.

#### Data Display
The server sends the filtered circulars back to the client-side, which then displays them in the UI. The UI updates in real-time to reflect the changes made by the user.

#### User Interaction
Users can interact with the UI to filter and view the circulars. For example, they can change the title, role, or date range, and the server updates the database accordingly.

### Summary
The "Circulars" page is a transactional feature that allows users to manage and track various circular communications. It exposes variables, parameters, and text grids to filter and display circulars based on specific criteria. The page passes information to the wider web system structure through form submission, server-side processing, and data display mechanisms.

### System Node Endpoint: https://learner.saveetha.in/rewards/my-reward-points/
### Analysis of the Student Portal Reward Points Section

#### 1. Action or Transactional Feature it Controls
The "My Reward Points" section in the student portal is designed to track and manage the reward points earned by students. This section allows students to view their reward points, credits, debits, and balance by reward term. The primary action it controls is the tracking and management of reward points, which are likely tied to specific achievements or milestones within the student's learning journey.

#### 2. Variables, Parameters, Text Grids, or Functions it Exposes
- **Reward Term**: This is a dropdown menu that allows students to filter their reward points by different terms. The terms could be categorized by different types of rewards or milestones.
- **Apply Button**: This button is used to apply the selected reward term and refresh the list of reward points.
- **Reward Policy**: This link likely provides information about the reward policy, which could include rules for earning, redeeming, or managing reward points.
- **Table Data**: The table displays the reward points, including the reward term, date, debit, credit, balance, and description. Each row in the table represents a specific reward point transaction.

#### 3. How this Page Passes Information to the Wider Web System Structure
- **URL Parameters**: The URL might include parameters such as `term` or `date` to filter the reward points displayed on the page.
- **JavaScript and AJAX**: The page likely uses JavaScript and AJAX to dynamically update the reward points table based on user interactions, such as selecting a reward term or applying a filter.
- **Server-Side Processing**: The page sends data to the server using AJAX requests when the "Apply" button is clicked. The server processes the request and returns the updated reward points data to the client.
- **Session Management**: The page might use session management to ensure that the user's reward points are tracked across different sessions, ensuring that the data is consistent and up-to-date.

### Detailed Breakdown of the Page Structure

#### HTML Structure
```html

<body>
    <header>
        <nav>
            <ul>
                <li><a href="#">Schedule</a></li>
                <li><a href="#">Circulars</a></li>

                <li><a href="#">Workflow Requests</a></li>
                <li><a href="#">Workflow Approvals</a></li>
                <li><a href="#">Bookings</a></li>

        <section id="reward-points">
            <h1>My Reward Points</h1>
            <p>Track your credits, debits, and balance by reward term.</p>
            <form>
                <label for="reward-term">Reward Term:</label>
                <select id="reward-term" name="reward-term">
                    <option value="All Terms">All Terms</option>
                    <!-- Other reward terms -->
                </select>
                <button type="button" onclick="applyFilter()">Apply</button>
                <a href="#" onclick="showRewardPolicy()">Reward Policy</a>

            <table id="reward-table">

                        <th>#</th>
                        <th>Reward Term</th>

                    </tr>
                </thead>
                <tbody id="reward-table-body">
                    <!-- Table rows will be dynamically populated here -->
                </tbody>
            </table>
        </section>
    </main>
    <footer>
        <p>Logged in as: 25013957</p>
        <p>MUHAMMAD ASJAD E</p>
        <p>Ref1: 212225240091</p>
        <p>Ref2: 25013957</p>
    </footer>
    <script src="scripts.js"></script>
</body>
</html>
```

#### JavaScript and AJAX
```javascript
document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', function() {
    const rewardTermSelect = document.getElementById('reward-term');
    const applyButton = document.querySelector('button[type="button"]');
    const rewardTableBody = document.getElementById('reward-table-body');

    applyButton.addEventListener('click', function() {
        const selectedTerm = rewardTermSelect.value;
        fetch('/api/reward-points', {
            method: 'POST',
            headers: {
                'Content-Type': 'application/json'
            },
            body: JSON.stringify({ term: selectedTerm })
        })
        .then(response => response.json())
        .then(data => {
            rewardTableBody.innerHTML = '';
            data.forEach(item => {
                const row = document.createElement('tr');
                row.innerHTML = `
                    <td>${item.id}</td>
                    <td>${item.term}</td>
                    <td>${item.date}</td>
                    <td>${item.debit}</td>
                    <td>${item.credit}</td>
                    <td>${item.balance}</td>
                    <td>${item.description}</td>
                `;
                rewardTableBody.appendChild(row);
            });
        });
    });

    rewardTermSelect.addEventListener('change', function() {
        const selectedTerm = rewardTermSelect.value;
        fetch('/api/reward-points', {
            method: 'POST',
            headers: {
                'Content-Type': 'application/json'
            },
            body: JSON.stringify({ term: selectedTerm })
        })
        .then(response => response.json())
        .then(data => {
            rewardTableBody.innerHTML = '';
            data.forEach(item => {
                const row = document.createElement('tr');
                row.innerHTML = `
                    <td>${item.id}</td>
                    <td>${item.term}</td>
                    <td>${item.date}</td>
                    <td>${item.debit}</td>
                    <td>${item.credit}</td>
                    <td>${item.balance}</td>
                    <td>${item.description}</td>
                `;
                rewardTableBody.appendChild(row);
            });
        });
    });
});
```

#### Server-Side Processing
The server-side processing would handle the request to fetch the reward points data based on the selected term. This could involve querying a database to retrieve the relevant reward points data and returning it to the client.

### Summary
The "My Reward Points" section in the student portal is designed to track and manage reward points earned by students. It exposes variables such as reward terms, applies filters, and updates the reward points table dynamically. The page communicates with the server using AJAX to fetch and update the reward points data, ensuring that the user's reward points are tracked and displayed accurately.

#### 1. Action or Transactional Feature it Controls

These requests are typically used for administrative purposes within the academic system, allowing students and administrators to manage and track the status of these requests.

#### 2. Variables, Parameters, Text Grids, or Functions it Exposes
- **Approval Status**: This dropdown allows users to filter the requests based on their approval status (e.g., "All," "Pending," "Approved," "Rejected").
- **From Date**: A date input field that allows users to specify the start date for the date range.
- **To Date**: Another date input field that allows users to specify the end date for the date range.
- **Apply Button**: This button applies the selected date range and filters to the list of requests.
- **General Letters**: A section that displays any general letters that have been sent or are pending.
- **Leave Applications**: A section that displays any leave applications that have been submitted or are pending.
- **Manual Academic Attendance**: A section that displays any manual academic attendance requests that have been submitted or are pending.

#### 3. How This Page Passes Information to the Wider Web System Structure
The "Workflow Requests" section likely interacts with the wider web system structure through various backend APIs or services. Here’s a breakdown of how this might work:

1. **User Interaction**: When a user selects a date range and clicks the "Apply" button, the system processes the selected date range and filters the requests accordingly.
2. **API Calls**: The system makes an API call to the backend to fetch the filtered list of requests based on the selected date range and approval status.
3. **Data Transfer**: The backend returns the filtered list of requests to the frontend, which then displays them on the page.
4. **User Interaction**: Users can then interact with the displayed requests, such as clicking on a specific request to view more details or initiate a new request.
5. **API Calls for Actions**: When a user initiates an action (e.g., sending a general letter, submitting a leave application), the system makes another API call to the backend to update the status of the request and possibly send it to the appropriate department or administrator.

### Example API Calls
1. **Fetch Requests by Date Range**:
   ```json
   {
     "method": "GET",
     "path": "/api/workflow_requests",
     "query": {
       "approval_status": "All",
       "from_date": "2026-08-09",
       "to_date": "2026-08-16"
     }
   }
   ```

2. **Submit Leave Application**:
   ```json
   {
     "method": "POST",
     "path": "/api/leave_applications",
     "data": {
       "student_id": "123456",
       "reason": "Sick leave",
       "start_date": "2026-08-10",
       "end_date": "2026-08-15"
     }
   }
   ```

3. **Send General Letter**:
   ```json
   {
     "method": "POST",
     "path": "/api/general_letters",
     "data": {
       "to": "admin@example.com",
       "subject": "General Letter",
       "content": "This is a general letter."
     }
   }
   ```

### Summary

- **Database Interaction**: The server interacts with the database to update the booking status and retrieve the updated booking information.
- **User Interface Update**: The server sends the updated booking information back to the client, which is then displayed on the user's screen.

   - The user selects the session time from the dropdown menu.

   - The form data is sent to the server via an HTTP POST request.

3. **Server-side Processing**:
   - The server receives the form data and processes it.
   - The server updates the booking status in the database.
   - The server sends the updated booking information back to the client.

4. **User Interface Update**:
   - The server sends the updated booking information back to the client.
   - The client updates the user interface to reflect the new booking status.

### Example of Form Submission and Server Response

#### 3. How This Page Passes Information to the Wider Web System Structure:

- **URL Parameters**: The URL parameters include the `reward_term` which is used to filter the reward points displayed.

- **Server-Side Logic**: The server-side logic processes the reward term selection and returns the appropriate reward points data to the client-side.
- **API Calls**: The page may make API calls to the server to fetch or update reward points data.

### Detailed Explanation of the Mechanisms:

1. **URL Parameters**:
   - The URL includes a parameter `reward_term` which is used to filter the reward points displayed. For example, if a student selects a specific reward term, the URL might look like this:
     ```
     https://learner.saveetha.in/rewards/my-reward-points/#?reward_term=1
     ```

2. **JavaScript**:
   - The page uses JavaScript to dynamically load and update the reward points table based on the selected reward term. This is achieved through event listeners and AJAX requests.
   - When a student selects a reward term, the JavaScript code updates the table with the corresponding reward points data.

3. **Server-Side Logic**:
   - The server-side logic processes the reward term selection and returns the appropriate reward points data to the client-side. This is typically done using a server-side framework like Node.js, Django, or Flask.
   - The server-side code might look something like this:
     ```python
     # Flask Example
     @app.route('/rewards/my-reward-points/<int:reward_term>')
     def get_reward_points(reward_term):
         # Fetch reward points data from database
         reward_points = get_reward_points_from_database(reward_term)
         return render_template('reward_points.html', reward_points=reward_points)
     ```

4. **API Calls**:
   - The page may make API calls to the server to fetch or update reward points data. This is done using HTTP requests, typically via AJAX.
   - The API might look something like this:
     ```javascript
     fetch('/api/reward_points', {
       method: 'GET',
       headers: {
         'Content-Type': 'application/json',
       },
       params: {
         reward_term: 1,
       }
     })
     .then(response => response.json())
     .then(data => {
       // Update the table with the fetched data
     });
     ```

By understanding these mechanisms, students can effectively manage their reward points, apply reward policies, and view detailed reward policy information within the student portal.

#### 3. How this Page Passes Information to the Wider Web System Structure
The form data is submitted via an HTTP POST request to the server. The form data is sent to the server using the `X-HTTP-Method-Override` header set to `POST` to simulate a GET request. This is a common technique used in web applications to handle form submissions without changing the URL.

The form data is then processed by the server-side script, which can be written in various programming languages such as PHP, Python, Java, etc. The server-side script can validate the form data, store it in a database, and send a response back to the client.

The response from the server can be handled by the client-side script using JavaScript, which can update the page or display a success message to the user.

### Example of the HTTP Request
Here is an example of the HTTP request that would be sent when the form is submitted:

```http
POST /academics/workflow-requests/leave-applications/create HTTP/1.1
Host: learner.saveetha.in
Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded
X-HTTP-Method-Override: POST

short_title=Short+Title&leave_type=Personal+Leave&leave_from=2026-08-17T08:00:00Z&leave_to=2026-08-17T16:00:00Z&subject=Reason+for+leave&attachment=example.pdf&content=This+is+a+long+description+for+the+leave+request.
```

### Summary

It exposes various form fields for the student to input details about the leave request, and the data is sent to the server for processing. The server-side script can then validate the data, store it in a database, and send a response back to the client.

  - `mentor_email`: The email of the mentor.
  - `mentee_email`: The email of the mentee.
  - `mentor_phone`: The phone number of the mentor.
  - `mentee_phone`: The phone number of the mentee.
  - `mentor_role`: The role of the mentor (e.g., mentor, mentee).
  - `mentee_role`: The role of the mentee (e.g., mentee, mentor).

- **Text Grids:**
  - Mentee details (name, email, phone, role).
  - Mentor details (name, email, phone, role).

- **Functions:**
  - `getMenteeDetails`: Fetches the details of the mentee.
  - `getMentorDetails`: Fetches the details of the mentor.
  - `assignMentor`: Assigns a mentor to a student.
  - `removeMentor`: Removes a mentor from a student.
  - `updateMentor`: Updates the details of a mentor.
  - `updateMentee`: Updates the details of a mentee.

#### 3. How This Page Passes Information to the Wider Web System Structure
- **HTTP Requests:**
  - The page makes an HTTP GET request to the server to fetch the mentee details.
  - The server processes the request and returns the mentee details in JSON format.
  - The page then parses the JSON response and displays the details.

- **Session Management:**
  - The page uses session management to store the logged-in student's ID and other session-specific data.
  - The server uses session data to authenticate the logged-in student and ensure that only authorized users can access the page.

- **API Calls:**
  - The page makes API calls to the server to perform actions such as assigning or removing mentors.
  - The server processes these API calls and updates the database accordingly.
  - The server then returns a response to the page, which is displayed to the user.

- **Cookies and Cookies:**
  - The page sets cookies to store session data and user preferences.
  - The server reads these cookies to authenticate the user and ensure that only authorized users can access the page.

### Summary
The "Mentees" section in the student portal is a critical feature that manages mentor-mentee relationships. It exposes variables, parameters, and functions related to mentor and mentee details, as well as actions such as assigning and removing mentors. The page communicates with the server using HTTP requests, session management, and API calls to update the database and display the information to the user.

- **Server-Side Processing**: The page communicates with the server to fetch the list of subjects for the selected term. This could involve making an AJAX request to a server-side endpoint that handles the request and returns the list of subjects.

```html
                <select id="term-select">
                    <option value="25-26-EVEN-T1-PLT">25-26-EVEN-T1-PLT</option>
                    <!-- Other terms -->
                </select>
            </div>
            <button id="apply-btn">Apply</button>
            <button id="reset-btn">Reset</button>
        </div>
```

#### JavaScript and AJAX
```javascript
document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', function() {
    const termSelect = document.getElementById('term-select');
    const applyBtn = document.getElementById('apply-btn');
    const resetBtn = document.getElementById('reset-btn');
    const subjectList = document.querySelector('.subject-list');

    termSelect.addEventListener('change', function() {
        const selectedTerm = termSelect.value;
        // Fetch subjects for the selected term using AJAX
        fetch(`/api/subjects?term=${selectedTerm}`)
            .then(response => response.json())
            .then(data => {
                if (data.length > 0) {
                    subjectList.innerHTML = '';
                    data.forEach(subject => {
                        const subjectElement = document.createElement('div');
                        subjectElement.textContent = subject;
                        subjectList.appendChild(subjectElement);
                    });
                } else {
                    subjectList.innerHTML = '<div>No subjects found for the selected term.</div>';
                }
            });
    });

    applyBtn.addEventListener('click', function() {
        // Apply the selected term and refresh the list
        // This could involve updating the term in the URL or making a new AJAX request
    });

    resetBtn.addEventListener('click', function() {
        // Reset the term selection
        // This could involve clearing the dropdown or setting it to the default term
    });
});
```

### Conclusion
The "Subjects Handling" section in the student portal is designed to manage and display the subjects assigned to the student for a specific academic term. It uses a dropdown to select the term, an "Apply" button to refresh the list of subjects, and a "Reset" button to clear the term selection. The page communicates with the server using AJAX to fetch the list of subjects for the selected term, ensuring a dynamic and responsive user experience.

### Analysis of the Student Portal Section

#### 1. Action or Transactional Feature

- **To Date**: A date input field that allows the user to specify the end date of the event session.
- **Apply Button**: A button that applies the selected filters and updates the displayed events.
- **Reset Button**: A button that resets the filters to their default state.

#### 3. How This Page Passes Information to the Wider Web System Structure
The page uses JavaScript to handle the form submission and update the displayed events. When the user clicks the "Apply" button, the form data is submitted to the server. The server processes the request and updates the database to reflect the selected filters. The updated data is then sent back to the client, and the page is refreshed to display the filtered events.

Here is a simplified representation of the process:

1. **User Selects Filters**: The user selects the event term, date range, and clicks "Apply."
2. **Form Submission**: The form data is submitted to the server using an AJAX request.
3. **Server Processing**: The server processes the request, updates the database, and returns the updated data.
4. **Page Refresh**: The page is refreshed to display the updated events.

### Detailed Steps
1. **User Interaction**:
   - The user selects an event term from the dropdown.
   - The user inputs the start and end dates in the date fields.
   - The user clicks the "Apply" button.

2. **AJAX Request**:
   - The form data is sent to the server using an AJAX request.
   - The request includes the selected event term, start date, and end date.

3. **Server Response**:
   - The server processes the request and updates the database.
   - The server returns the updated data to the client.

4. **Page Update**:
   - The page is refreshed to display the updated events.

### Example AJAX Request
```javascript
// Example AJAX request to apply filters
$.ajax({
    url: '/events-managing',
    type: 'POST',
    data: {
        event_term: $('#event-term').val(),
        from_date: $('#from-date').val(),
        to_date: $('#to-date').val()
    },
    success: function(response) {
        // Update the page with the new events
        $('#event-list').html(response);
    },
    error: function() {
        // Handle error
    }
});
```

### Conclusion

```javascript
  // Fetch feedback status
  fetch('/api/feedback/status')
```

```python
@app.route('/api/feedback', methods=['POST'])
def feedback():
    data = request.json
    student_id = data['student_id']
    feedback = data['feedback']
    timestamp = datetime.now()
    db.session.add(Feedback(student_id, feedback, timestamp))
    db.session.commit()
    return jsonify({'status': 'success'})
```

  - `time_end`: End time of the class.
  - `student_id`: Unique identifier for the student.

- **Parameters:**
  - `IN OTP`: Entry One Time Password.
  - `OUT OTP`: Exit One Time Password.
  - `IN OTP Entry`: Time when the student entered the class.
  - `OUT OTP Entry`: Time when the student exited the class.

- **Text Grids:**
  - `IN OTP`: Text indicating where the student can find their IN OTP.
  - `OUT OTP`: Text indicating where the student can find their OUT OTP.
  - `Lesson`: Title of the lesson being taught.
  - `Topics`: List of topics covered in the lesson.
  - `Class Started`: Indicates when the class started.
  - `Pending`: Status indicating if the entry or exit is pending.

- **Functions:**
  - `Generate OTP`: Function to generate the IN OTP and OUT OTP.
  - `Check OTP`: Function to verify the entered OTP.
  - `Log Entry/Exit`: Function to log the student's entry and exit times.

#### 3. How this Page Passes Information to the Wider Web System Structure
- **Cookies and Sessions:**
  - The page uses cookies to store session data, such as the student's ID and the session's unique identifier.
  - Sessions are used to maintain the state of the page and the user's progress.

- **API Calls:**
  - The page makes API calls to the backend to generate OTPs, check OTPs, and log entries and exits.
  - These API calls are made using HTTP requests, typically via JavaScript or a similar technology.

- **Database Interaction:**
  - The page interacts with a database to store and retrieve student attendance data.
  - This database might be a relational database like MySQL or a NoSQL database like MongoDB.

- **Security Measures:**
  - The page uses HTTPS to ensure secure communication between the client and the server.
  - It employs CSRF tokens to prevent cross-site request forgery attacks.
  - Passwords are hashed and stored securely using a hashing algorithm like bcrypt.

### System Node Endpoint: https://learner.saveetha.in/academics/people_schedule/?year=2026&month=8&day=20
### Analysis of the Student Portal Section

#### 1. Action or Transactional Feature
This section controls the display of the student's academic schedule for the month of August 2026. It allows students to view their upcoming classes, lessons, and their attendance status for each session.

#### 2. Variables, Parameters, Text Grids, or Functions Exposed
- **Year**: 2026
- **Month**: August
- **Day**: 20
- **Schedule**: Displays the student's schedule for the selected day.
- **Circulars**: Displays any circulars or announcements related to the student's schedule.
- **My Reward Points**: Displays the student's reward points.
- **Workflow Requests**: Displays any workflow requests or pending tasks.
- **Workflow Approvals**: Displays any workflow approvals or pending tasks.
- **Bookings**: Displays any bookings or reservations.
- **Services**: Displays any available services or resources.
- **Mentees**: Displays any mentees or mentees' schedules.
- **Mentorship**: Displays any mentorship or mentorship-related information.
- **Logged in as**: Displays the logged-in user's information, including their ID, name, and referrer details.

#### 3. How This Page Passes Information to the Wider Web System Structure
- **URL Parameters**: The page is accessed via a URL with parameters such as `year`, `month`, and `day`. For example:
  - `https://learner.saveetha.in/academics/people_schedule/?year=2026&month=8&day=20`
  - These parameters are used to filter the schedule based on the selected year, month, and day.
- **JavaScript and AJAX**: The page likely uses JavaScript and AJAX to fetch and display the schedule data dynamically. This ensures that the page updates without a full page reload.
- **Server-Side Processing**: The server-side code processes the request and returns the appropriate schedule data to the client-side JavaScript.
- **Session Management**: The page may use session management to ensure that the logged-in user's schedule is displayed correctly, even if they navigate away from the page and come back later.

### Detailed Explanation

1. **URL Parameters**:
   - `year`: Specifies the academic year (e.g., 2026).
   - `month`: Specifies the month (e.g., August).
   - `day`: Specifies the day (e.g., 20).

2. **JavaScript and AJAX**:
   - The page uses JavaScript to handle the dynamic loading of schedule data.
   - AJAX (Asynchronous JavaScript and XML) is likely used to fetch the schedule data from the server without reloading the entire page.
   - The JavaScript code might use event listeners to handle user interactions, such as clicking on a date to view the schedule.

3. **Server-Side Processing**:
   - The server-side code processes the request and returns the appropriate schedule data.
   - This data is then passed back to the client-side JavaScript, which updates the DOM to display the schedule.

4. **Session Management**:
   - The page uses session management to ensure that the logged-in user's schedule is displayed correctly.
   - This is important to maintain the user's session and ensure that their schedule is up-to-date even if they navigate away from the page and come back later.

### Example of AJAX Request
```javascript
// Example of an AJAX request to fetch the schedule
fetch('/academics/people_schedule', {
  method: 'GET',
  headers: {
    'Content-Type': 'application/json',
    'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + localStorage.getItem('token')
  },
  params: {
    year: '2026',
    month: '8',
    day: '20'
  }
})
.then(response => response.json())
.then(data => {
  // Update the DOM with the fetched schedule data
  document.getElementById('schedule').innerHTML = data.schedule;
  document.getElementById('circulars').innerHTML = data.circulars;
  document.getElementById('rewardPoints').innerHTML = data.rewardPoints;
  // Add event listeners for user interactions
  document.getElementById('schedule').addEventListener('click', handleScheduleClick);
  document.getElementById('circulars').addEventListener('click', handleCircularClick);
  // Add more event listeners as needed
})
.catch(error => console.error('Error:', error));
```

### Conclusion
This section of the student portal is designed to provide a user-friendly interface for students to view their academic schedules, manage their rewards, and access other relevant information. It leverages AJAX for dynamic updates and session management to ensure that the schedule data is always up-to-date and accessible to the logged-in user.
